 [2404.06271] On the Goncharov’s conjecture in degree 𝑚-1 and weight 𝑚 On the Goncharov’s conjecture in degree m − 1 𝑚 1 m-1 and weight m 𝑚 m Vasily Bolbachan 1 1 1 This paper is an output of a research project implemented as part of the Basic Research Program at the National Research University Higher School of Economics (HSE University). This paper was also supported in part by the contest ”Young Russian Mathematics” Abstract Let 𝕂 𝕂 \mathbb{K} be a field of characteristic zero. We prove that its motivic cohomology in degree m − 1 𝑚 1 m-1 and weight m 𝑚 m is rationally isomorphic to the cohomology of the polylogarithmic complex. This gives a partial extension of A. Suslin theorem describing the indecomposable K 3 subscript 𝐾 3 K_{3} of a field. Goncharov formulated the following conjecture: Conjecture 1.1 (Goncharov’s conjecture) . For any field 𝕂 𝕂 \mathbb{K} there is a functorial isomorphism H i , m ​ ( Spec ⁡ 𝕂 , ℚ ) ≅ H i ​ ( Γ ​ ( 𝕂 , m ) ) superscript 𝐻 𝑖 𝑚 Spec 𝕂 ℚ superscript 𝐻 𝑖 Γ 𝕂 𝑚 H^{i,m}(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{K},\mathbb{Q})\cong H^{i}(\Gamma(\mathbb{K},m)) . The conjecture 1.1 was formulated almost 30 years ago, however it is known only in a very few cases: when i = m 𝑖 𝑚 i=m [ Tot92 , NS90 ] and when i = 1 , m = 2 formulae-sequence 𝑖 1 𝑚 2 i=1,m=2 [ Sus91 ] . We remark that both of these cases were known before [ Gon95 ] . The goal of this paper is to prove the following theorem: Theorem 1.2 . The Conjecture 1.1 is true when i = m − 1 𝑖 𝑚 1 i=m-1 and m ∈ ℤ , m ≥ 2 formulae-sequence 𝑚 ℤ 𝑚 2 m\in\mathbb{Z},m\geq 2 . Let us give a more explicit version of this theorem. We use the cubical version of higher Chow groups. Denote by □ n = ( ℙ 1 \ { 1 } ) n superscript □ 𝑛 superscript \ superscript ℙ 1 1 𝑛 \square^{n}=({\mathbb{P}}^{1}\backslash\{1\})^{n} the algebraic cube of dimension n 𝑛 n . Subvarieties of X × □ n 𝑋 superscript □ 𝑛 X\times\square^{n} given by the equations of the form z i = 0 , ∞ subscript 𝑧 𝑖 0 z_{i}=0,\infty are called faces. The higher Chow groups are the cohomology of a certain complex 𝒵 ​ ( X , m ) 𝒵 𝑋 𝑚 \mathcal{Z}(X,m) . The group 𝒵 ( X , m ) ) j \mathcal{Z}(X,m))_{j} is a quotient of the group of codimension m 𝑚 m algebraic cycles on X × □ 2 ​ m − j 𝑋 superscript □ 2 𝑚 𝑗 X\times\square^{2m-j} intersecting all faces properly by the subgroup of degenerate cycles [ BK94 ] . The differential is given by the signed sum of restrictions to codimension one faces. Here is a more precise version of our main result: Theorem 1.3 . Let 𝕂 𝕂 \mathbb{K} be a field of characteristic zero and m ≥ 2 𝑚 2 m\geq 2 . The map 𝒯 𝒯 \mathcal{T} is a quasi-isomorphism. When 𝕂 𝕂 \mathbb{K} is algebraically closed, this map is an isomorphism. 1.1 The idea of the proof According to [ Rud21 , Blo86 ] on both complexes there is a norm map with good properties. This reduces the general case to the case when 𝕂 𝕂 \mathbb{K} is algebraically closed.
